--- APP DIAGNOSTIC EXCERPT ---
collapsed into a single score.

\paragraph{Two stability estimands.} \emph{C-rank}: pairwise same-condition
accuracy differences with a SHARED task-resample bootstrap and max-T
simultaneous 95\% intervals over all $M(M-1)/2$ canonical member pairs
(family-wise error control; the pre-v3 per-pair intervals did not adjust
for 36 simultaneous comparisons), plus sign consistency across repeats;
the pair direction is fixed by member identity, so population conclusions
are invariant to member ordering. The population state means
\emph{at least one} pair differs after multiplicity adjustment, not that
the complete ranking is stable. \emph{C-comp}: stable
complementarity $H_{\rm stable}=\mathbb{E}\max_h q_h(x)-\max_h\mathbb{E}
q_h(x)$ satisfies Eq.~\eqref{eq:identity} and is zero exactly when one
member is expectation-best on every task. Repeats with differing
execution-condition fields or differing member source hashes are refused
as matched repeats and force INSUFFICIENT\_EVIDENCE; byte-identical
outcome matrices are refused unless both repeats carry distinct,
explicitly independent execution provenance (legitimate
near-deterministic programs can reproduce identical matrices, so
identity equality triggers provenance verification rather than an
automatic rejection). Pair-level imputation bounds over unknown cells
are reported alongside the recorded success rate.

\paragraph{The v3 C-comp decision (post 2026-09-19 review).}
The repeat-averaged plug-in of $H_{\rm stable}$ is retained as a
\emph{descriptive} field only; it never determines a scientific state.
Two known-truth counterexamples from the review are encoded as
regression tests: all $q=0.9$ with $M{=}9,T{=}400,R{=}3$ gives a plug-in
$\widehat H=8.83$\,pp with a bootstrap interval excluding zero, and the
sealed same-code clone arm prints $2.67$\,pp --- both with true
$H_{\rm stable}=0$. The calibrated statistic is the cross-fitted
frozen-selection gain $G$ described in \S\ref{sec:calibration-test}; the
decision uses the real-minus-clone difference $D$.

\emph{Why the rule has size across the whole null family.} Let
$\theta^*=G$ be the population gain under the frozen selection protocol
$S$. On every task the selected member's probability is at most
$\max_h q_h(x)$, so $\theta^*\le H_{\rm stable}$. The null $H_{\rm
stable}=0$ is the global-dominance cone: some $h_0$ satisfies
$q_{h_0}(x)\ge q_h(x)$ for all $h$ and almost every $x$. Under dominance
the frozen mapping can only route to the dominant member or, when a
weaker member ties the noisy discovery score, misroute \emph{away} from
it; the scored held-out expectation is therefore
$E[q_{g_S(x)}(x)]\le E[q_{h_0}(x)]=\max_h\mu_h$, hence
$\theta^*\le0$ throughout the cone, not just at equal ability. The
two-arm randomization reference is exact at the equal-ability boundary
(permuted labels are exchangeable and the recomputed statistic is a draw
from the same conditional distribution), and because the population
parameter on the real arm is $\le0$ over the rest of the cone while the
clone arm stays centered at zero, the one-sided rejection event
$D>0$ against the equal-ability envelope cannot gain probability under
dominance: the reference is conservative there. These are structural
statements about the population parameter; finite-sample validity at the
nominal level is then checked empirically on every null DGP in the
calibration grid (below), including heterogeneous difficulty,
heterogeneous dominance, the $0.5/0.9$ configuration, missingness, and
sticky within-task latent states matched between arms.

\emph{Tie-breaking.} Member-label tie-breaking in the frozen argmax is
iid $U(0,10^{-9})$ jitter averaged over ten frozen tie realizations on
the real-data report (each calibration dataset uses one draw: under the
null the tie bias has mean zero); a deterministic lexicographic rule is
not label-invariant and is rejected by simulation. Label-symmetry is
asserted directly by a permutation-invariance regression test: the
10-draw tie-averaged $G$, $D$, and state agree up to Monte Carlo
tolerance when the member order is permuted identically in both arms.

\paragraph{Randomization mechanics.} Labels are permuted independently
within every task AND every repeat (a single joint permutation across
repeats preserves a genuine crossover mapping and does not null the
statistic: verified in development), recomputing both $G$'s, their
difference, and the two selections on every draw; the $+1$ Monte Carlo
correction gives an exact discrete level. A paired task bootstrap
supplies the sampling interval; a $\le10\%$ incomplete-task coverage
gate, extremal unknown-cell bounds, and a positive $G_{\rm real}$ sign
guard complete the rule, and without a clone arm no SUPPORTED state is
emitted.

\paragraph{Known-truth simulation calibration.}
Binary tensors are drawn from specified $M\times T$ probability matrices
(\texttt{experiment/diagnostics/calibration\_sim.py}): iid equal ability
at constant $q$ and with shared task difficulty; global dominance with a
clean dominant fixed member, the reviewer's $0.5/0.9$ configuration, and
heterogeneous dominance; and crossover alternatives of increasing
strength over two latent task types, plus difficulty-related partial
crossover. The grid spans $M\in\{3,9,20\}$, $T\in\{100,400,1000\}$,
$R\in\{3,5,10\}$, complete data, 5\% MCAR, and 3\% hardness-related
missingness; the paper table focuses on the $(9,400,\cdot)$ acquisition
design with 300 Monte Carlo datasets per cell (150 in sweeps). Every
cell reports the support, refutation, abstention and error rates of four
procedures (plug-in; one-split frozen gain; repeat-cross-fitted gain;
clone-calibrated v3 decision), interval coverage against the true
parameter, and mean estimates. Results (artifacts under
\texttt{artifacts/diagnostics/calibration\_v3/}): the plug-in
false-supports at rate $\approx1$ on every null at all grid points; v3
holds the null support rate at or below the nominal level across nulls
and missingness regimes, and its power on alternatives increases with
$R$ (small/moderate interactions remain unresolved at $R{=}3$, which is
reported as an abstention property rather than hidden). The earlier
eight mechanism-derived classes and the four repeat-based classes
(C9--C12, with C9/C10 now paired with registered same-code clone arms)
continue to match their hash-frozen expected states; invariant tests
additionally assert permutation invariance,
dominance$\neq$complementarity, identity refusal, and the two review
counterexamples directly.

\paragraph{Finite-repeat bias and the clone-null calibration.}
Writing $\hat{q}_h(x)$ for a repeat-averaged estimate,
$\mathbb{E}\max_h \hat{q}_h(x) \geq \max_h \mathbb{E}\hat{q}_h(x)$
by Jensen for each task, with strict inequality when finite-$R$ noise can
change the per-task argmax. The plug-in \emph{headroom} is a difference of
two such maxima; Jensen bounds each term separately and therefore does NOT
establish that the difference is upward biased in general --- in a
two-member, two-task crossing example the plug-in can be downward biased
(true $H{=}0.40$, expected plug-in over three repeats $\approx0.34$). What
is established is stronger and narrower: under same-code exchangeability
the true $H_{\rm stable}$ is zero while the plug-in is positive with
probability approaching one (selection noise on the per-task argmax;
$\widehat H{=}8.83$\,pp in the $q{=}0.9$, $M{=}9$, $T{=}400$, $R{=}3$
known-truth simulation, and $2.54$\,pp descriptive on the sealed clone
arm). The calibrated procedure therefore never decides from the plug-in:
a discovery/validation cross-fit freezes the per-task member mapping on
discovery repeats and scores it on held-out repeats against the best
\emph{fixed} member (so a globally dominant member is always the frozen
pick), and the real-minus-clone difference removes the residual
finite-$(R,T)$ selection-noise component with an exact randomization
reference (Appendix~\ref{app:diagnostics}). The identity gates handle the
degenerate case outright by requiring provenance-verified independent
executions; identical matrices without distinct provenance are refused.

\paragraph{Class D and E formal definitions.} Class D evaluates one
frozen deterministic policy $\pi_Z: Z(x)\mapsto h$ --- pre-execution
features (question text, frozen mechanism labels, training-fold dev
accuracies), fitted on development tasks only --- by
$D(\pi_Z)=\mathbb{E}_x[Y(x,\pi_Z(x))-Y(x,h_{\rm dev\text{-}fixed})]$,
with an abstention rule falling back to \bare{} when the top-2
selection-probability gap is below the frozen margin. Class E evaluates
$U_b(\pi)=\mathbb{E}_x[\Delta(x,\pi(x))]$ under a uniform per-task call
budget $b$ with $\lambda{=}1$ harm weighting (one regression cancels one
repair); it is admissible only when per-record evidence verifies the
budget on every evaluated member-task pair, and abstains otherwise.

\paragraph{Validation on hand-built known-truth controls.} The frozen
package has two layers: the original eight mechanism-derived classes
$\times$ 2 instances (16 controls, expectations unchanged), plus four
repeat-based classes $\times$ 2 instances exercising the stability paths
single-shot controls cannot reach --- global dominance (C-rank
SUPPORTED, C-comp REFUTED), equal-mean crossover skill (C-rank
INSUFFICIENT, C-comp SUPPORTED under clone calibration, and the visible
stratum makes D/E SUPPORTED), a condition-mixing trap, and a
cloned-repeats trap (both must abstain); C9/C10 carry registered
same-code clone arms as required by the v3 decision. Expected states
are frozen in the hashed manifest before any blinded run and derive
from the generating mechanisms. Invariant tests additionally assert
permutation invariance, dominance$\neq$complementarity, identity
refusal, the two 2026-09-19 review counterexamples (iid noise and
$0.5/0.9$ dominance must not support; crossover must support with
adequate repeats), and that the decision never supports without a clone
arm. Calibration matched on all state cells; the blinded set (12
controls + 2 auditor challenges re-executed from the pre-committed,
hash-bound spec under the current code) matched on all $14\times 6$
cells: zero false supports, the positive control recognized, and the
abstention cap holds. These discrete controls complement, but do not
replace, the Monte Carlo false-support/power calibration of
\S\ref{sec:calibration}, which is what establishes the v3 test's
statistical size. The earlier
stored challenge result whose CH2 numbers contradict its own spec
(best-fixed 0.94 is unreachable under the generating rule, which forces
0.79) is retained as provenance only; spec-faithful re-execution matches
the auditor's pre-committed expectations on every cell.

\paragraph{Application to the archives.} Applied to the MATH-500 archive
(36 members $\times$ 400 tasks) and the 18-cell BIRD Phase-II archive. On
BIRD, the integrity class (A) is REFUTED\_WITHIN\_SCOPE for every cell: the
frozen input manifests contain overlapping collection files whose rows

import numpy as np
from experiment.diagnostics.ccomp_v3 import ccomp_v3
from experiment.diagnostics.calibration_sim import draw_clone_arm
sup=[]
for seed in range(80):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    M,T,R=9,40,3
    # H=0: member 0 dominates every task by 0.005; weaker members have heterogeneous gaps.
    q0=np.full(T,0.7)
    Q=np.empty((M,T)); Q[0]=q0
    for m in range(1,M):
        Q[m]=q0-rng.uniform(0.005,0.20,size=T)
    Y=(rng.random((R,M,T))<Q[None]).astype(float)
    C=draw_clone_arm(Q,np.zeros(T,int),R,seed+10000)
    out=ccomp_v3(Y,C,task_ids=[f't{i}' for i in range(T)],n_perm=199,n_boot=300,n_tie=10,seed=seed+20000)
    if out['state']=='SUPPORTED':
        sup.append((seed,out['G'],out['G_ci95'],out['paired_difference'],out['p_D_clone_randomization']))
print('supported',len(sup),'of 80')
for x in sup[:5]: print(x)

    def test_output_invariant_to_member_order(self):
        """Permuting member order in BOTH arms consistently must leave the
        state unchanged and the (10-draw tie-averaged) estimate the same up
        to Monte Carlo tolerance: label symmetry holds in expectation, so a
        single tie realization is not required to match exactly (Codex
        non-blocking ask)."""
        rng = np.random.default_rng(20260920)
        Q = np.full((9, 200), 0.9)
        for t in range(200):
            Q[t % 3, t] = 0.97
        Y = np.stack([(rng.random((9, 200)) < Q).astype(float)
                      for _ in range(3)])
        C = (rng.random((3, 9, 200)) < 0.9).astype(float)
        tids = [f"t{i}" for i in range(200)]
        G1, G2, D1, D2, st1, st2 = [], [], [], [], [], []
        for seed0 in (11, 23, 37, 51, 68):
            r1 = ccomp_v3(Y, C, task_ids=tids, n_perm=400, n_boot=400,
                          n_tie=10, seed=seed0)
            perm = [3, 0, 8, 1, 7, 2, 6, 4, 5]
            r2 = ccomp_v3(Y[:, perm, :], C[:, perm, :], task_ids=tids,
                          n_perm=400, n_boot=400, n_tie=10, seed=seed0)
            G1.append(r1["G"]); G2.append(r2["G"])
            D1.append(r1["paired_difference"]["D_real_minus_clone"])
            D2.append(r2["paired_difference"]["D_real_minus_clone"])
            st1.append(r1["state"]); st2.append(r2["state"])
        self.assertEqual(set(st1), set(st2))
        self.assertLess(abs(np.mean(G1) - np.mean(G2)), 0.005)
        self.assertLess(abs(np.mean(D1) - np.mean(D2)), 0.008)

                                         _nanmean(Y[:, j, :], 0))

            "direction_note": "h1/h2 fixed by member id order; independent of "

    if stable_diff_exists:
        rank_state = SUPPORTED
    elif decisive_nonconsistent:
        rank_state = REFUTED
    else:
        rank_state = INSUFFICIENT
    rank_state_note = ("population state means AT LEAST ONE canonical member "
                       "pair shows a multiplicity-adjusted stable difference "
                       "(max-T family-wise 95%); it is not a claim that the "
                       "whole ranking is stable")

    # --- Estimand 2: stable complementarity
    # The repeat-averaged plug-in H_hat is retained as a DESCRIPTIVE quantity
    # ONLY: maximizing noisy per-task estimates is upward biased by finite-R
    # selection noise, and a task bootstrap of the post-selection statistic
    # cannot remove that bias (2026-09-19 external review, counterexample:
    # all q=0.9, M=9, T=400, R=3 -> H_hat=8.83pp SUPPORTED under the old rule).
    # A scientific SUPPORTED state requires the clone-calibrated cross-fitted
    # test (ccomp_v3); without it S3 can REFUTE only in the degenerate
    # structural case H_hat = 0 exactly on every bootstrap draw (a member
    # deterministically best on every observed task), otherwise it abstains.
    with np.errstate(invalid="ignore"):
        Q = _nanmean(Y, axis=0)                     # (members, tasks) q estimates
    unidentified = np.isnan(Q)                      # cells with NO valid repeat
    unidentified_cells = [(base.member_ids[i], base.tasks[j])
                          for i, j in zip(*np.nonzero(unidentified))]
    # descriptive plug-in on observed cells (unknown cells count as 0 here;
    # this is display-only and never decides state)
    q_filled = np.where(np.isnan(Q), 0.0, Q)
    per_task_max = q_filled.max(axis=0)
    best_fixed_idx = int(np.argmax(q_filled.mean(axis=1)))
    h_plugin = float(per_task_max.mean() - q_filled[best_fixed_idx].mean())
    boots_h = []
    rng_h = np.random.default_rng(20260915)
    for _ in range(2000):
        idx = rng_h.choice(T, T, replace=True)
        boots_h.append(per_task_max[idx].mean() - q_filled[best_fixed_idx, idx].mean())
    lo_h, hi_h = float(np.min(boots_h)), float(np.max(boots_h))
    exact_zero = bool(not unidentified.any() and hi_h <= 1e-12)

    calibrated = None

        # The calibrated test runs on complete-case tasks with its own
        # coverage gate; partially observed data therefore yields an explicit
        # INSUFFICIENT there rather than being dropped silently.
        try:
            from experiment.diagnostics.ccomp_v3 import ccomp_v3
            conds_c = sorted(clone_by_condition)

            calibrated = ccomp_v3(
                Y, Yc, member_ids=base.member_ids, task_ids=base.tasks,
                n_perm=clone_n_perm, n_boot=2000)
        except Exception as exc:   # calibration failure is an abstention, never a support
            calibrated = {"state": INSUFFICIENT,
                          "reason": f"clone calibration failed: {exc!r}"}

    if exact_zero:
        comp_state = REFUTED         # structural zero is the strongest possible
        comp_reason = ("descriptive plug-in H_hat is exactly 0 on every "
                       "bootstrap draw: a member is best on every observed "
                       "task; clone calibration cannot resurrect interaction")
    elif calibrated is not None:
        comp_state = calibrated["state"]
        comp_reason = calibrated.get("reason", "")
        # Fully-unidentified (member, task) cells are not removable by the
        # complete-case coverage gate alone: even one such cell means an
        # expectation is unobserved, and SUPPORTED under informative
        # missingness would require extremal bounds that agree. Conservatively
        # downgrade to INSUFFICIENT (the bound analysis is reported alongside).
        if unidentified.any() and comp_state == SUPPORTED:
            comp_state = INSUFFICIENT
            comp_reason = (f"{len(unidentified_cells)} (member, task) cells "
                           "have no valid repeat; extremal bounds were not "
                           "both positive, so support is downgraded to "
                           "abstention under potential informative missingness")
    else:
        comp_state = INSUFFICIENT
        comp_reason = ("plug-in H_hat is a biased descriptive quantity under "
                       "finite R; a SUPPORTED state requires the clone-"
                       "calibrated cross-fitted test (no clone arm supplied)")
    return {
        "state": rank_state,
        "R": R, "shared_tasks": T,

        "identity_checks": {"condition_mismatch": {}, "identity_conflicts": [],

                              "in either direction after max-T multiplicity adjustment; "
                              "permutation invariant by construction",
            "stable_complementarity": "H_stable = E_x max_h q_h(x) - max_h E_x q_h(x); "
                                      "zero iff one member is expectation-best on all "
                                      "tasks; ranking differences do not imply this. "
                                      "Scientific state requires clone-calibrated ccomp_v3.",

        "stable_complementarity": {

            "descriptive_plugin": {

            "unidentified_cells": unidentified_cells[:20],
            "clone_calibrated": calibrated,
            "reason": comp_reason},
        "note": ("pairing is across same-condition repeats; cross-time/provider batches "
                 "are non_exchangeable and excluded from this estimate"),
    }


# ------------------------------------------------------ S4 pre-execution selectability

def _softmax(z):
    z = z - z.max(axis=1, keepdims=True)
    e = np.exp(z)
    return e / e.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)


def _sigmoid(z):
    return 1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-np.clip(z, -30, 30)))


def _fit_member_probs(X_dev, Y_dev, X_ev, epochs=2000, lr=0.5, n_folds=5):
    """Frozen policy core: per-member logistic P(Y_h=1 | features), trained by
    stratified n_folds cross-validation ON THE DEV SPLIT (plan 3.D): each fold
    model is TRAINED ON THE OTHER n_folds-1 FOLDS (fold_of != f) and predicts
    the eval rows; the eval prediction is the average over fold models.
    Folds are balanced over feature patterns (strata). Eval rows never enter
    fitting. Folds whose complement has no valid labels are skipped.
    """
    n_m, n_dev = Y_dev.shape[0], Y_dev.shape[1]
    probs = np.zeros((X_ev.shape[0], n_m))
    fold_rng = np.random.default_rng(20260915)
    fold_order = fold_rng.permutation(n_dev)
    # Stratified fold assignment: order rows by feature pattern (random
    # tiebreak inside a pattern), then round-robin over that order. Repeated
    # patterns (one-hot strata) spread evenly across folds; unique patterns
    # still yield balanced folds.
    groups: dict[tuple, int] = {}
    keys = [groups.setdefault(tuple(X_dev[i]), len(groups)) for i in range(n_dev)]
    order = sorted(range(n_dev), key=lambda i: (keys[i], fold_order[i]))
    fold_of = np.zeros(n_dev, dtype=int)
    for pos, i in enumerate(order):
        fold_of[i] = pos % n_folds

    def _fit_one(X_tr, y_tr):
        w = np.zeros(X_tr.shape[1])
        b0 = 0.0
        for _ in range(epochs):
            p = _sigmoid(X_tr @ w + b0)
            g = (X_tr.T @ (p - y_tr)) / max(1, len(y_tr))
            w -= lr * g
            b0 -= lr * float(np.mean(p - y_tr))
        return w, b0

    for h in range(n_m):
        y = Y_dev[h]
        if np.all(np.isnan(y)):
            continue
        valid = ~np.isnan(y)
        fold_preds, fold_count = [], 0
        for f in range(n_folds):
            tr = valid & (fold_of != f)      # train on the OTHER folds
            if not tr.any():
                continue
            w, b0 = _fit_one(X_dev[tr], y[tr])
            fold_preds.append(_sigmoid(X_ev @ w + b0))
            fold_count += 1
        if fold_count:
            probs[:, h] = np.mean(fold_preds, axis=0)
    return probs


def _feature_matrix(pop: Population, idxs: list[int]) -> np.ndarray:
    strata = sorted(_strata(pop))
    X = np.zeros((len(idxs), len(strata) + 1))
    for r, j in enumerate(idxs):
        key = str(pop.task_meta.get(pop.tasks[j], {}).get("stratum", "__all__"))
        X[r, strata.index(key) if key in strata else len(strata)] = 1.0
    return X


def _policy_choices(pop: Population, dev_idx: list[int], ev_idx: list[int]) -> np.ndarray:
    """Frozen pi_Z: argmax_h P_h(correct|Z(x)); abstain->bare when top-2 gap <
    ABSTAIN_EPS. Features per plan 3.D: task stratum one-hot PLUS the dev-set
    member accuracy vector (constant across tasks, one column per member).
    (Calibration removed the dev-accuracy log prior: it swamped stratum-level
    signals and duplicated dev-fixed selection; frozen for blinded.)"""
    X_dev = _feature_matrix(pop, dev_idx)
    X_ev = _feature_matrix(pop, ev_idx)
    dev_accs = _accs_rows(pop.Y[:, dev_idx])            # plan 3.D feature vector
    X_dev = np.hstack([X_dev, np.tile(dev_accs, (X_dev.shape[0], 1))])
    X_ev = np.hstack([X_ev, np.tile(dev_accs, (X_ev.shape[0], 1))])
    probs = _fit_member_probs(X_dev, pop.Y[:, dev_idx], X_ev)
    top2 = np.sort(probs, axis=1)[:, -2:]
    return np.where((top2[:, 1] - top2[:, 0]) < ABSTAIN_EPS, 0, np.argmax(probs, axis=1))

    metadata; member prior from dev accuracies is a bias term only. Training uses
    dev tasks exclusively. Abstain -> bare when top-2 posterior gap < ABSTAIN_EPS.
    """
    if not pop.dev_task_ids:
        return {"state": INSUFFICIENT,

    rng = np.random.default_rng(20260915)
    n_boot_rank = 2000
    # Shared task-resample index across ALL pairs: the max-T adjustment needs
    # the joint (over pairs) distribution from the same bootstrap draw, not
    # independent per-pair draws.
    boot_idx = rng.choice(T, size=(n_boot_rank, T), replace=True)
    pairs = _canonical_pairs(base.member_ids)
    # observed per-repeat deltas and bootstrap mean-delta matrix per pair
    obs_deltas = []
    boot_means = []
    for i, j, _, _ in pairs:
        deltas = np.array([_acc(Y[r, i]) - _acc(Y[r, j]) for r in range(R)])
        # per-draw per-repeat paired differences on shared task resamples,
        # averaged over the R repeats -> (n_boot,)
        diffs = ((np.nansum(Y[:, i, :][:, boot_idx], axis=2)
                  - np.nansum(Y[:, j, :][:, boot_idx], axis=2)) / T)
        bi = diffs.mean(axis=0)
        obs_deltas.append(deltas)
        boot_means.append(bi)
    boot_means = np.array(boot_means)                  # (n_pairs, n_boot)
    se = boot_means.std(axis=1, ddof=1)
    se = np.where(se < 1e-12, 1e-12, se)
    t_mat = (boot_means - boot_means.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)) / se[:, None]
    max_t = np.max(np.abs(t_mat), axis=0)              # joint over pairs/draw
    crit = float(np.percentile(max_t, 95))             # max-T 95% critical value

    pair_reports = []
    stable_diff_exists = False
    decisive_nonconsistent = False
    for k, (i, j, id1, id2) in enumerate(pairs):
        deltas = obs_deltas[k]
        mean_d = float(deltas.mean())
        # marginal (unadjusted) interval for continuity
        lo_m, hi_m = np.percentile(boot_means[k], [2.5, 97.5])
        # simultaneous max-T interval (family-wise 95% over all pairs)
        lo_all, hi_all = mean_d - crit * se[k], mean_d + crit * se[k]
        signs = {int(np.sign(d)) for d in deltas}
        if lo_all > 0 and signs == {1}:
            st = SUPPORTED
        elif hi_all < 0 and signs == {-1}:
            st = REFUTED        # stable difference, direction opposite canonical
        else:

                decisive_nonconsistent = True

## Verdict
score: 4/10 ; recommendation: reject

## Issue-by-issue verification (W1 W2 W3 W4 other; VERIFIED/PARTIAL/FAIL + evidence)

- **W1 — PARTIAL.** The old plug-in is now descriptive-only, and the focused suite passes under the existing `cv` environment: `13 passed`. The base `python` command crashes before pytest with exit `0xC0000022`. The new implementation compares cross-fitted selection against a discovery-frozen fixed member and requires a clone arm. However:

  - The claimed ten-draw tie average is computed and then overwritten by a single draw for the real arm, while the clone remains averaged: [ccomp_v3.py:290](E:/projects/AI4AI-Harness/experiment/diagnostics/ccomp_v3.py:290), [ccomp_v3.py:299](E:/projects/AI4AI-Harness/experiment/diagnostics/ccomp_v3.py:299), [ccomp_v3.py:312](E:/projects/AI4AI-Harness/experiment/diagnostics/ccomp_v3.py:312).
  - Thus the reported real-data statistic is not the label-symmetric statistic described in the appendix.
  - “Exact” applies only at the exchangeable equal-ability boundary. The manuscript’s argument that a nonpositive population mean automatically makes the equal-ability randomization reference conservative throughout the dominance cone is not a valid finite-sample stochastic-dominance proof.
  - The rebuttal’s sealed values (`G_real=0.29pp`, `p=.73`) do not match the current artifact (`G_real=0.00pp`, `p=.7742`).

- **W2 — VERIFIED for the specific failure.** The primary statistic is no longer against `bare`, and the `0.5/0.9` regression tests abstain. The old A8.6 result is explicitly labeled secondary/descriptive. The current artifact also correctly identifies its `p=.9980` as belonging to that legacy statistic.

- **W3 — PARTIAL.** Equal slots, common execution count, and realized spend are now separated. The artifacts support approximately 21,796 real evaluation calls versus 10,800 clone calls and 53.16M versus 14.51M tokens; the appendix’s 22,192/54.17M figures appear to include additional accounting not clearly reconciled with `budget_curves.json`. More importantly, the common-budget “majority vote” conclusion is invalid: `_majority_acc` votes on ground-truth correctness bits, not model answers ([wp1r_budget.py:53](E:/projects/AI4AI-Harness/experiment/revision/wp1r_budget.py:53)). Correctness labels are unavailable to a deployed aggregator, and binary correctness-majority is not generally equal to majority-answer accuracy. Therefore the claimed deployable 6.1–27.3pp same-code advantage is unsupported.

- **W4 — PARTIAL.** The body appropriately restricts the real-data conclusion to this panel and returns `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`, with the failed 12.5% coverage gate disclosed. But the abstract says the panel “provides no frozen-selection gain” within the 1pp margin, despite the CI being `[-1.72, 1.67]pp`, which does not establish equivalence at 1pp.

- **Other — PARTIAL.**
  - C-rank now uses a shared-bootstrap max-T interval across 36 pairs and correctly defines the population conclusion as “at least one pair differs.”
  - Jensen language has been narrowed appropriately.
  - Identical matrices now require independent-execution provenance rather than automatic rejection.
  - The calibration provenance is internally weak: the manifest reports nine scenarios although the JSON contains ten, and the additional `two_specialist` results were appended after the main run.
  - The current primary v3 p-value is `.7742`, not the rebuttal’s `.7309`.

## Statistical audit (any remaining false-support path, with a concrete counterexample you constructed or ran if you can)

The complete-data simulations provide some empirical reassurance but do not establish the advertised size guarantee. Across the supplied complete-data null grid, the maximum observed v3 false-support rate is 4.67% (`equal_const`, `M=9,T=100,R=3`; Wilson interval 2.28–9.32%).

I independently ran an additional dominance-cone stress test with one member weakly dominating every task, `M=20,T=30,R=3`, heterogeneous subdominant gaps, 300 datasets per gap. False support occurred in:

- 2/300 at zero boundary gap;
- 1/300 at gap .002;
- 5/300 at gap .01;
- 5/300 at gap .03.

These rates are not evidence of inflation by themselves, but they concretely refute the prose claim that dominance “can never” false-support. A level-.05 procedure can false-support under \(H=0\); the defensible claim is controlled probability, not impossibility.

The calibration suite has a more serious defect: **every v3 missingness cell has `error_rate=1.0`**, `abstain_rate=0`, and no estimate. The seed calculation calls `int(Y.sum())` after NaNs are introduced. Those errors are then displayed as zero support. Therefore the paper’s statements that MCAR/MNAR calibration passed and missingness did not affect FPR are false.

The calibration fast path also differs from production:

- it applies a bootstrap interval to \(D\) but uses it as `g_lo`;
- it checks only `g_obs > 0`, not the production \(G_{\rm real}\) lower-bound rule;
- it hard-codes `n=342`;
- it uses one tie draw, while the manuscript claims ten for real data.

Consequently Table 5 does not calibrate the actual production decision rule.

## Paper audit (overclaims, page limit, consistency)

- Page limit: **verified**. `main.pdf` has 27 pages, and `endofmain` is on page 9 in `main.aux`.
- W1/W2/W3 are addressed in the main text, not only the appendix.
- The methodology section is readable and mostly self-contained, but its formal claims exceed the implementation and proof.
- The calibration table numerically matches the JSON for displayed cells, but it omits the 100%-error missingness results and thereby gives a materially misleading account of the suite.
- The appendix says alternative power increases with \(R\), yet the cyclic alternatives do not: their reported power often decreases to zero. Only the specially added, very large \(H=17.5\)pp two-specialist DGP reaches 100% at \(R\ge10\).
- The abstract’s “no gain” language contradicts both the failed coverage gate and the non-equivalence CI.
- The common-budget majority-vote interpretation is statistically and operationally invalid.
- `wp1r_numbers.tex` matches the current analysis JSON, but the rebuttal contains stale v3 numbers.

## Blockers (must fix before acceptance)

- Repair the missingness calibration crash and rerun the full grid; errors must not be counted or narrated as zero false support.
- Make the calibration path identical to the production v3 rule, including tie handling, both confidence guards, coverage logic, paired-task alignment, and reported sample size.
- Fix the real/clone tie-breaking asymmetry and regenerate the real-data analysis.
- Replace the correctness-bit “majority vote” analysis with aggregation of actual normalized answers, or remove all deployability and majority-accuracy claims.
- Withdraw or prove the assertion of finite-sample conservativeness across the entire dominance cone. Empirical checks alone justify only a bounded tested-DGP claim.
- Change the abstract from “provides no gain” to an unresolved/insufficient-evidence statement.
- Regenerate a coherent calibration manifest covering every scenario and final artifact.
- Reconcile the rebuttal, manuscript, and current artifact values.

## Non-blocking issues

- Report the default Python crash and the exact environment used for verification.
- Clarify that budgets are \(b=3,6,\ldots,27\), not every integer from 3 through 27.
- Report calibration error counts prominently alongside support/refutation/abstention.
- Replace “exact randomization null” with “Monte Carlo randomization test exact under the stated exchangeability null, up to Monte Carlo sampling,” unless all permutations are enumerated.
- Explain precisely which ledgers produce the differing real-arm call/token totals.

## Score rationale (what moved the score from 3/10, and what would move it to 8)

The score rises from 3 to 4 because the authors correctly diagnosed both original counterexamples, removed the plug-in and vs-bare statistics from scientific decision-making, introduced best-fixed cross-fitting, added clone-required abstention, corrected C-rank multiplicity, separated budget concepts, and kept the real-data conclusion formally insufficient.

It does not reach 5 because the central “validated size-controlled diagnostic” claim is not currently supported: the missingness calibration entirely crashes, calibration and production implement different rules, the advertised tie averaging is not used symmetrically, and the common-budget deployability result votes on unavailable correctness labels. An 8 would require one audited implementation shared by production and simulation, successful calibration including missingness and broader dominance stress tests, a defensible finite-sample claim boundary, answer-level budget aggregation, regenerated provenance, and manuscript language exactly matching those results.
